Until she was face-down again, cheek pressed to the familiar nothing. Home position. Safe
position. Maybe-she'll-go-away-again position.
Maybe if I just stay very, very still, she'll get bored again.
It worked once. It can work twice. ...Right?
"Hmm..."
{REDACTED}'s voice drifted over, thoughtful now.
"You know what? This is perfect actually! You being all quiet and still means I can catch you
up on EVERYTHING without interruptions! It's like talking to the Cosmos's best listener!"
More fabric rustling. Springs creaking that shouldn't exist.
"So! While I was gone—which was quite far, thank you for asking—I saw the most
interesting things! Did you know that if you fold space just right, you can see colors that don't
exist? Well, they exist, but also don't, which is the fun part! Oh! And I practiced making
temporal loops shaped like butterflies, but they kept turning into moths, which is basically the
same thing but sadder..."
***'s jaw clenched until her teeth ached.
This was going to be a very, very long not-sleep.
"And then...mhmmm...Let me think, let me think...what else could I talk about? A thousand
years is sooo much time! Even for me!" A pause. Sound of fingers drumming against knees."Oh! Oh! I know!"
*** felt the floor vibrate slightly as {REDACTED} bounced in place, still cross-legged but
using her whole body like a spring.
"Remember when I said I changed my name? Well, there's a story there!"
She waited exactly three seconds, probably with hands cupped around her mouth like sharing
a secret.
"No? Nothing? Okay, I'll tell you anyway!"
More rustling. Swish of arms being thrown wide.
"So you know how I can do time thingies, right? Make moments stretch or squeeze or skip?
Well, turns out that's SUPER useful for races! The other star-spawn brats would line up at the
starting line, all tentacles and teeth and 'my daddy controls a whole nebula' this and 'my
mommy births a thousand young' that, and then—"
—SIIIIIUUUUUU!
It started as a whisper and ended as an echo, like someone had ripped the middle out of a
shout and stitched the ends together.
"See? Like that!”
A small thud as {REDACTED}'s palms slapped the floor in triumph.
“I’d be at the finish line before little Zothy even lifted his first pseudopod!"
She giggled, miming running with just her arms.
"Oh, they got SO mad! 'Cheater!' they'd scream—" Her voice pitched high and whiny.
"'That's not fair!' they'd whine. Bunch of sore losers!"
Sound of hands gesturing wildly, cutting through air.
"But here's the thing—" A finger jabbed toward ***'s general direction. "—why shouldn't I
use what I was born with? That's like telling Mynoghra not to use her dream-walking or
telling the Thousand Young not to use their... thousand-ness. It's dumb!"
***'s fingers twitched slightly.
She can do what she wants and nothing breaks.
No one screams. No one dies.
Lucky.
“ANYWAY! They started calling me names. The popular one was 'Sue' because of the sound
—'there goes Sue!' they'd say, all mocking-like: 'Siiiuuuu-girl is cheating again!'" A pause."But you know what? I LOVED it!"
Arms thrown up in victory from the sound of air displacement.
"Sue! Short and sweet and nothing like…. like what we were called before. So I kept it!
Made it mine! Now when they say 'Sue,' they're not mocking me. They're saying MY name.
The one I chose!"
She leaned back, the soft thud of palms hitting floor behind her for support.
"Showed them AND got rid of any reminder of... her."
The cheerfulness in her voice suddenly cracked like thin ice.
"...You know, it's funny. In some ways, everyone here—all us 'little nuisances' as the Old
Geezers call us—we've all got parent issues."
A bitter laugh followed, one hand gesturing vaguely upward.
"Daddy is too busy playing watchdog. That's just how he is, right? Important universe stuff,
blah blah blah....But Mother? She's got time to birth thousands of writhing spawn… but not
for—"
She cut herself off. A sharp inhale.
"...Not for us."
A hand suddenly poked ***'s shoulder, not gentle, but not harsh either.
"...Hey. You still awake under there? Just checking!"
The cheerful tone was was back, painted thick over the cracks.
"Anyway! That's how I became Sue! Pretty cool story, right? Right!"
Another poke, this one more insistent.
"...I'm gonna assume that silence means 'Yes, Sue, that's the BEST story, please tell me more
about your awesome adventures!'"
No. Please….No more stories.
*** cracked her eyes open just enough to scan the void.
Need….block the noise...
Nothing but darkness and... there. The pile of books.
"Well, since you asked SO nicely..."Sue crossed her arms with theatrical satisfaction, puffed out her chest like a proud pigeon,
and closed her eyes to better savor her moment of victory.
In the same moment, *** moved.
Swift and silent as the Cheshire Cat, she pushed up from the floor and silently darted toward
the pile.
"—I have TONS more stories about how I became the most amazing—Oh...?"
Sue's eyes popped open to find only empty floor where *** had been.
"***?"
***’s  hands moved quickly, grabbing the first few tomes they found—The Ugly Duckling,
Jack and the Beanstalk, The Brave Little Tailor.
The covers felt slightly worn under her fingers, edges gone soft from Mom's handling.
Sorry… This isn't what you're for, but...
She laid back down on her stomach, carefully arranging the books around her head like a
paper fortress. Two pressed against her ears, one draped over the back of her head like a hat.
Through the book-filter however, Sue's voice came dampened but still unfortunately audible:
"Gee...where do you think you're going?”
Through the corner of her eye—the books left her peripheral vision clear—*** saw a Mary
Jane about to land squarely on The Fox and the Grapes.
Her foot shot out, sliding the book away with her toes just before impact. That airhead of a
Sue didn't notice of course, too busy crouching down to examine ***’s fortress with genuine
curiosity.
"...Huh. So these are what humans call ‘books’....”
She picked one up, turning it over in her hands like examining a strange artifact.
"I mean, I've seen them before, obviously, but never actually..."
Sound of a book being opened.
A pause.
Then noises of flipping—sideways from the sound of pages hitting wrong.
"How do you even—"
More rustling."Oh, this way? No, wait—"
Finally, a satisfied ‘Ah!’
“….’The Tor-toise and the Ha-re’…”
Soon came more noises of rapid page-flipping—
—fwip-fwip-fwip-fwip—
“…How can anyone look at this all day? There's barely any drawing! Just all these little..."
—fwip-fwip-fwip—
"...squiggly thingies! Look, this whole page is just—wait, is that supposed to be an 'S' or a
snake? Why humans put tiny snakes everywhere? Are they stupid?”
A pause. *** felt warmth as Sue leaned closer to her makeshift book-helmet, probably
squinting at the titles.
"Please tell me you didn't waste the last thousand years on this lame stuff. There's SO many
more exciting things to do than staring at silly little scribbles all day!”
It's the closest thing a monster can be to happiness…
"Oh, wait, here's a picture!" Sue had stopped flipping pages, pointing an illustation on the
right side of the left page. "Look at this—a smug-looking rabbit-thing and a sad lumpy turtle-
thing. I don't know what they're supposed to be doing exactly..."
A thoughtful hum vibrated through the floor.
“…Actually, you know what? They kinda look like us! See, the rabbit's all confident and
shiny—that's obviously me—and the turtle's all hiding in its shell being a grump—that's
you!”
At least the turtle has a shell to hide in.
"So here's what happens: The awesome rabbit finds the boring turtle just sitting on a beach
doing NOTHING, right? And she's like 'Hey! Let's go on an adventure!' But the turtle's all
'Noooo, leave me alone, I want to sit here and be sandy forever!'"
***'s fingers scraped slightly against the floor. The itch was starting again, prickly and
insistent, crawling up her spine.
"But the rabbit's too smart to give up! She grabs the turtle and drags her all the way to this
amazing forest with, like, rainbow trees and singing flowers and racing tracks made of
starlight!"
Sue's voice grew animated, probably gesturing wildly even though *** couldn't see."At first the turtle keeps trying to hide in her shell, complaining the WHOLE way—'This is
too bright!' 'This is too loud!' 'My shell hurts!' Whine, whine, whine! But then... the turtle
peeks out. Just a little. And she sees how beautiful everything is. How much she's been
missing by staying on that stupid beach. And she tells the rabbit 'I'm sorry I doubted you. You
were right. Thank you for not giving up on me.'"
A pause. When she spoke again, her voice was quieter….
….Hopeful?
"And then they race through the forest together, and the turtle discovers she's actually really
fast when she tries, and they have the best day ever, and neither of them are ever lonely
again. The end!"
She closed the book with a satisfied snap that echoed in the void.
"See? Much better than whatever those hairless monkeys scribbled inside! And way more
realistic too! Happy endings are the only endings worth having!"
If only it were that simple…
"...What's so great about lying like that anyway?"
A soft thump filled the air. Through the corner of her eye, *** saw her twin lying on her
stomach—but wrong. Arms at weird angles like broken doll parts, one leg bent up at the
knee, the other splayed sideways.
Even lying still, Sue vibrated with barely-contained energy. Her fingers drummed against the
floor—
—tap-tap-tap.
Her feet flexed and pointed. Then her legs began to scissor slowly, left-right, left-right, like
she was swimming through air.
"Okay... lying down... being boring..."
Her arms started moving too, up-down, up-down, as if making snow angels in the void. One
eye popped open to peek at ***.
"...Is this the part where I start having gloomy thoughts? What do you even think about when
you're like this?”
Ten seconds. Her movements sped up. Legs kicking faster, arms flapping like a grounded
bird.
"Oh! I know! We're gloom buddies now! Two peas in a sad, sad pod! The Melancholy
Sisters! The Sorrowful Siblings! The—"Fifteen seconds. Sue's shadow had started twitching independently from her body, and her
movements were becoming erratic—legs bicycling, arms windmilling.
Twenty seconds. She was practically vibrating now, her form blurring at the edges like a
hummingbird trying to pretend it was a sloth.
Twenty-five seconds—
"THIS IS SO BORING!"
She exploded upward like a jack-in-the-box, bouncing straight up and landing perfectly on
her Mary Janes with a click.
"There's nothing fun about lying here like a dead fish! How do you DO this?! My everything
itches! I need to move!"
She started pacing in tight circles, gesturing wildly, arms cutting through the air.
"I'm trying to understand, you know? Really trying! What makes you all..." She waved
vaguely at ***'s still form. "...melancholy and still and UN-FUN? So I can fix it! But this is
impossible!"
"Are you done yet...?"
*** had reached her limit. The constant chatter, the movement, the sheer exhausting presence
of someone who refused to just leave her alone—
“Just... go away... please..."
Sue stopped mid-pace, one foot still raised. For once, blessed silence fell.
It stretched. And stretched.
Long enough that *** almost dared hope—
"Ehi...Speaking of, um, names... I was thinking... have you changed yours too?"
A name? Me?
Of all things...
“….You know, like I did? Got rid of the old one, picked something new?"
A pause.
"...No? Nothing? Okay, well, I have IDEAS!"
Of course she would.
“There's Sarah—that's a human one! Very simple, rolls off the tongue!" She counted on her
fingers, bouncing slightly with each name. "Or maybe Lucy? That means 'light' which isironic since you like the dark but irony is fun! Ooh, or Emma! That's like, the most popular
human girl name ever, you'd blend right in!"
Sue was building momentum now, spinning as she listed.
"Or we could go fancy—Victoria means 'victory'! You'd be claiming triumph over... whatever
you're triumphing over! Lying down? Or there's Rose, which is pretty but has thorns, very
you! Or—"
"I don't want a new name."
Sue's mouth hung open mid-word, frozen mid-spin.
"I don't deserve one... Monsters don't get cute names. They get called what they are."
Sue stood perfectly still for perhaps the first time since arriving—not even her fingers moved.
“…Monster?"
*** wanted to bash her head against the floor again.
Stupid. Stupid, stupid, stupid.
Now Sue would dig and dig and dig, gentle as an elephant among glass jars but persistent as
the tide.
“…Well, I mean, yeah, our true forms are all tentacles and too many eyes and whatever… not
the best thing to look at all day… but you're my sister! You're—"
Sue seemed to struggle for words, hands gesturing more carefully now.
"...Even supposing you were a monster, which you're not but for the sake of argument let's
say yes... so what?" Her voice gained confidence. ”Who says a monster can't have a pretty
name? Who made that rule?"
Just let me sleep. Just let me forget.
Stop making it harder to do what needs to be done.
"...Actually, you know what? If you really were a monster—which again, you're not—but if
you were, wouldn't that make having a pretty name even MORE important? Like... like a
balance thing?"
*** squeezed her eyes shut and said nothing. Silence stretched. Sue shifted. The bouncing
lightness draining away drop by drop.
"You know... I was actually happy at first. I saw you lying there in that cute human form and
thought—finally! *** figured it out! *** pulled herself together!" A bitter laugh, nothing like
her usual giggles. "Silly me, right? Thinking you'd actually..."Her words hang out in the air. When Sue continued, her voice had gone eerily calm. Too
calm. Like the silence before lightning strikes.
"...Were they humans? Did they call you with that word? ‘Monster’?”
'Devil's spawn!'
'Demon!'
'Monster!'
*** shook her head against the void-floor, hard enough to hurt.
"N-no..."
"Don't lie!"
Sue's form flickered—for a split second she was there-not-there-there again, like a candle
flame in wind. Her edges blurred, snapping back to focus.
"I KNOW you went to Earth! Father dragged me from a gathering with other Star-spawns—
do you know how embarrassing that was? Him just appearing, grabbing me by the scruff like
I'm some misbehaving pup?"
Her fists clenched, knuckles white.
"Shows me you all unconscious and wouldn't say what happened! Just kept calling it a 'minor
incident' like I'm stupid! Said I had to 'monitor' you but wouldn't tell me why!"
Another flicker-blur of her outline, there for a heartbeat then solid again.
“But I can't just 'monitor' you like that asshole with a stick up his butt said!"
Her form stuttered again—multiple Sues overlapping for an instant before collapsing back to
one.
"I can't bear seeing you like this, just... rotting away! Not again!" Her voice cracked on the
last word. "I'm not making the same mistake twice!"
Sue lunged forward. Small arms wrapped around ***'s shoulders, hauling her up with
strength that belonged to their true forms.
"And I'm NOT going to sit here and watch you rot away because I was too weak and coward
to—to—"
Sue plopped down directly in front of her. All pretense of playfulness gone.
"GAH! I'm done playing around! You hear me? Done sugarcoating! So spill the beans! What
happened down there? Did those hairless monkeys scare you THAT much? Was it their weird
breathing? Their inside-wetness? The way they leak from their faces when they're sad?"*** stared, caught off-guard by this version of her twin.
She's... sad? Like me?
Quickly, she lowered her gaze to avoid that burning stare.
“N-no…”
“THEN WHAT????”
Sue seized ***'s wrists, pulling them forward and clasping ***'s hands between her palms.
The grip was warm, gentle but unyielding—like being held by someone afraid you'll
disappear if they let go.
“…Did they not like how you look? Did someone steal your shoes? Did they make you eat
their terrible food? What could possibly—"
"I don't want to talk about it."
*** kept her eyes fixed on their joined hands, Sue's fingers trembling slightly against hers.
"I just....want to forget..."
Sue groaned, loud and frustrated, her form doing that flicker-thing again—edges blurring
with emotion before snapping back.
"I'm trying my BEST to help! But without a teensy bit of collaboration on your end, it's
impossible! Im-poss-i-ble!" Her voice broke on the last word and her form flickered
violently, multiple versions of her overlapping—child-Sue, tentacle-Sue, something in
between—before stabilizing.
A sign left her lips then, and slowly, she released ***'s hands.
“…If... if it's too painful to say... maybe I can read your mind instead?"
*** jerked slightly, muscles tensing. She turned to stare.
"What?"
"Read your mind. Duh! Telepathic communication or whatever. Old man Daoloth once said
this was how we interacted with each other, before we discovered humans and got all fancy
with vocal cords."
Sue scooted closer, her hands leaving ***'s lap to hover over her head. Her shadow did that
wrong thing again, reaching before she did, eager and grasping.
"...Can I? Please?"
Her other hand rose in a solemn gesture, fingers spread wide."I promise—pinky swear, cross my hearts and hope to die—I'll leave you alone after.
Complete silence. Total void-quiet. Just... let me understand?"
*** stared at the hovering hand.
The solution was so simple it almost made her laugh.
Or cry. She wasn't sure which anymore.
She'll see.
See what I did. See the bodies.
See Agalika.
And then she'll finally understand what I am.
And then she'll leave forever.
Just like Mom, just like...
The void around them seemed to lean in, waiting. Sue's hand trembled just slightly, betraying
nervousness her voice didn't show.
But isn't that what I want? To be left alone? To never hurt anyone again?
"...Yes."
Sue's hand settled on ***'s scalp immediately. Fingers spread against ***'s hair, and her eyes
—when *** dared peek—brimmed with swirling energy, like galaxies being born and dying
all at once.
A second later *** felt a presence sliding through her thoughts like fingers through water. It
wasn’t Not painful per se, just... strange. Foreign. It felt like being turned inside out.
She tried to think of nothing, to build walls of emptiness, but the memories surged up like
vomit—
—Agalika teaching her to write on her notebook—
—The sharp crack of the cursed tool splitting the air—
—"MY NAME IS—!"
—Bodies falling like broken dolls—
—Monster monster monster MONSTER—
What felt like hours compressed into heartbeats later, Sue pulled her hand back. The absence
left ***'s scalp tingling, phantom warmth lingering where fingers had been. Her twin's eyesshone with something *** couldn't read—not the disgust she'd expected, not the fear she'd
counted on.
Just... neutral. Flat.
Before *** could voice anything, Sue stood abruptly, brushing invisible dust from her dress
with quick, efficient movements.
"Welp! As promised, I'll go away now. Forever and ever, just like you wanted!"
She turned on her heel—a normal turn, no dramatics—and started walking. No backward
glances, no "really-really leaving this time!" No skip in her step or drag in her feet.
Why didn't she—?
Where's the horror? The anger?
Why did she look so—
"Hmm...What a shame though..." Sue rocked back on her heels, hands clasped behind her
back. "...But oh well….Guess I'll just keep it for myself where her soul went then~♪"
***’s body moved on its own, scrambling upright so fast the void spun. Her legs, unused to
standing after so long lying like a corpse, wobbled like a newborn fawn's. Muscles screamed
in protest, tendons tight as old rope.
Three staggering steps—each one threatening to be her last—and she reached Sue, her hand
shooting out to grip her twin's shoulder, spinning her around with strength she didn't know
she still had.
"You know...? Where... where is—"
Sue's grin spread slow and wide as a cheshire cat's, all teeth and satisfaction.
"Oh yes~♪ I know EXACTLY where Agalika-something's soul flew away to." 
She lifted one hand, examining her fingernails with exaggerated interest, buffing them
against her dress in lazy circles. 
"But you're sooooo busy being a sad little crybaby, wallowing in your void-puddle of self-
pity, and I JUST promised I'd leave you alone forever for good, so..." She shrugged. "Why
should it matter?"
***'s other hand joined the first, gripping both of Sue's shoulders now, fingers digging in like
she could squeeze the answer out.
"Please! Please, tell me where—"
With a casual motion—almost lazy—Sue stepped backward, flicking ***'s hands off like
they were particularly annoying insects."My my my, what a FASCINATING development!” She spun in place, dress flaring,
absolutely radiating satisfaction. "Turns out all it took was the right motivation~♪”
"Please... I need to... I need to fix...She helped me and I... I broke her and..."
If I could find Agalika's soul, I could put it back.
Like placing a heart back in a clockwork doll.
Like kissing a sleeping princess awake.
Sue tapped her chin with one finger, rolling her eyes up as if consulting the void itself for
answers.
She rocked on her heels—forward, back, forward, back—
"Wellllll...I suppose…since you're asking SO nicely..."
Her hand shot out, quick as thought, grabbing ***'s wrist.
"Come on then, you lazy crybaby. Let's go find your human's soul. But you better keep up—
I'm not slowing down for any more moping!"
She tugged, and *** stumbled forward. Her legs protested, weak from disuse, muscles like
wet paper.
Two steps, three—
Her knees buckled, the strength that desperation had lent finally running out.
She pitched forward, the void rushing up to meet her—
But Sue was already there, catching her before she could fall.
"Geez...When I said keep up, I didn't mean face-plant immediately~!
But her hands were gentle as she steadied ***, patient as she waited for her twin to find her
balance again.
“…Can you walk? Or do I need to drag you the whole way?"
Without waiting for an answer, Sue quickly changed idea, spun around and crouched down,
pulling ***'s arms over her shoulders.
"Up we go!"
In one swift motion, she hoisted *** onto her back, small hands gripping under her twin's
knees. *** found herself suddenly elevated, the void looking different from this new height.
"W-wait... hold—""Nope~That's where the fun begins!”
She took off running. The acceleration knocked the breath from ***’s lungs as the void
rushed past, her long hair streaming behind them like a black banner. Their forms grew
smaller, two dark specks racing toward some unseen horizon.
The gust from their departure sent ripples through the space they'd left behind, disturbing the
pile of abandoned books.
One tumbled over, pages fluttering. *The Elves and the Shoemaker* fell open to an
illustration: a small, energetic elf carrying another, more somber one on his back, both
grinning as they raced through the night to help a poor soul in need.
Chapter End Notes
Howdy! To everyone who took an interested to reach this point....welp, first of all, sorry
for two months radio silence from me.
The last chapter ended in a way that was an hard reset in many aspects, and future
chapters building upon it would require a sort of hard reset in many places i grew
familiar until that point, and for a long time the ideas and sketches to set the beginning
of the following arc didn't satisfy me, and combined with my stubborness to get out the
best i can do led me to a creative impasse, which combined with the constant that is IRL
led to this.
...Until now!
I recently tweaked a few things of my daily routine, and i was suddenly hit with a divine
revelation that sparked my creative juices once more, which led me to finally get over
with the ninth chapter and the basic plot for the next following two ones.
So yeah, that's about it.
Hope you liked the direction I decided to go with after the wreckage of the previous
chapter, and if you have anything to say about it feel free to leave a comment as always.
And with that i'll take my leave and get back into the basement and hope for more
sparks of inspirations to iron out the next chapter...hopefully they'll come faster this
time...
Edit: Ah! And before i forget, i've also updated the previous chapters, changing the
alicespeak words for normal one for a better reading ease, added a few little minor
things here and there and overall put a new coat of paint, so if you have time on your
disposal, consider giving then a look!Chapter 10: What Is Not My Problem
Chapter Notes
See the end of the chapter for notes
The white boundary hums against me.
Not a sound one hears with ears——Does this vessel even possess such thing? I forgot—
rather a vibration that exists in more dimensions than this form currently occupies.
I trace one fingernail—or what passes for a fingernail when I bother with such pedestrian
shapes—along its surface.
Nothing. Not resistance, not recognition. The wall neither yields nor notice my touch, really,
which is almost insulting.
Am I THAT intangible?
Or is it that am I so uninteresting?
Above, below, through angles that shouldn't exist in three-dimensional space, white streams
converge, a sight to behold even for the likes of me.
They flow within the white wall with the inevitability of moths to flame, yet never quite
touch it, pooling and eddy at its edges instead, creating patterns that would have surely drove
foolish mortals who swore their meager existence in pursuit of knowledge to beautiful, ever
so amusing madness.
Heh…A shame they’d surely die on the way.
….Now what?
That wannabe Charon takes his ‘custodial duties’ so very seriously, guarding the great
melting pot of life as if they were the last drops of wine in creation, forgetting that the finest
vintages are meant to be drunk, not merely cellared.
And yet his garden is, I must begrudgingly admit, an irritatingly well-made fortress.
Credit where it's due, I suppose…
Not that I lack the strength to simply…‘crack’ it open. Quite the contrary.
But the amount of raw, unsubtle force required would light up this sector of spacetime like a
cosmic scream, drawing the attention of every boring, self-important consciousness within
ten light-years.
It rather defeats the purpose of subtlety, doesn't it?And I do so prefer subtlety.
….Usually.
Not that I have much choice either. The very last thing I need is to draw the direct, humorless
attention of the Keys Bearer and end up sharing a forced vacation with that cunning elk
goddess myself.
Speaking of the devil…
That cunning vixen and that so-called ‘secret factor’ she hinted at back then… her purported
"game-changer" for our kind... a knowledge for me still denied…
Until I retrieve and hold for her a specific object from within this cosmic vault, that is.
Better for her that this is worthy. Or else, If this proves to be nothing more than a waste of
time….I’ll be still very much in a position to take a…
Bite.
...Yes, a 'bite'.
Out of her.
I begin to move—a sliding sort of motion that's neither walking nor floating—around the
barrier's circumference.
The streams slightly part before me like frightened goldfishes, and I savour their fear-tinged
sweetness. Even as mindless spiritual residue, humans know to avoid me.
Smart of them.
Perhaps another angle will reveal weaknesses this one doesn't—
—Hm?
I glance behind.
Are those footsteps I just—?
No. Not footsteps. Rather a dissonant pluck on the strings of causality, emanating from…
everywhere.
….Something is coming.
And it doesn't seem to care much for the universe's established speed limits.
Interesting. To say the least.
Without nothing more than a fleeting thought, I pour my substance back into the gaps
between moments, becoming just another shadow in a ocean made of them.Let's see who is in such a desperate hurry to reach this particular nowhere.
And more importantly—let’s see if they can save me the trouble of getting my hands dirty.
—Or what pass for hands....Or dirty, for that matter.
Ugh…Details, details…
 
 
Wind that wasn't wind screamed past her.
Colors that had no names blazed and shrieked against her squeezed-shut eyelids—unnamed
shades, bitter and sweet all at once, their hues spinning, twisting, bleeding—
Her bandages trailed behind her, ghostly ribbons caught by thorns of nothingness—
Everything halted. The universe took a sharp breath in and held it.
But ***’s fragile human body misses the cue—peeling away from her sister’s back like an
autumn leaf, tumbling helplessly into the hungry dark.
She expected pain, a cruel kiss from the cold floor—but instead she lands softly.
….Softly?
Softly, like the gingerbread house's walls must have felt to Hansel and Gretel—warm and
welcoming.
For a heartbeat she sank into that yielding embrace, wrapped tight in something both vast and
gentle—
It snapped back. Spitting her out just like the whale had expelled Pinocchio and Geppetto
from its belly, limbs flailing, pinwheeling—twisting against nothingness—
"Wheee~!"
Until Sue's hands caught her mid-flight—small but strong, absorbing the momentum,
pirouetting with *** clutched to her chest.
"That was perfect-perfect!
Round and round they whirled, ***’s twin delighted laughter rang out like church bells, until
finally they slowed to a stop and Sue set *** down.
“Did you see-see-see how far you flew, little sis~? The speed! The bounce! Such a bounce—
marvelous~!”“Ghh…!”
***’s world kept tilting though they'd stopped. Her insides had become a waterlogged
sponge.
Never...
Again...
“Anyway-anyway,” Sue chirped, one arm still steadying ***’s trembling shoulders, pointing
upward with theatrical flair. “Look-look-look! Aren’t they lovely~?”

down.

It was working.

I’m going to be flung to the end of everything…

—

They buckled, giving way completely, and she crashed back down onto the mirror with a soft
grunt.
She tried again, gritting her teeth, pushing with all her willpower.
The result was worse, her arms barely lifting her an inch before collapsing again.
Useless.
With a tired sign, *** let her head rest on the cool, unforgiving floor, her breathing slowing
into ragged, exhausted sighs.
Just… just five minutes of rest…
Please hold on…
Sue… Agalika…
just…five…
….minutes…
 
 ***’s eyes fluttered open.
She felt… not rested, not really, but perhaps less like a frayed string about to snap. The dull
throb in her head had subsided to a faint echo.
How much… did i sleep?
Pushing herself up slowly as to avoid any muscle protesting, she sat on the floor and rubbed
the sleep from her eyes, only to freeze, hands still pressed to her face.
The infinite, sterile silver was gone.
She was in… a corridor?
A long, narrow hallway with dark, polished wooden floors and wallpaper patterned with
faded, drooping roses. Ornate mirrors in heavy, gilded frames were hung at intervals along
both walls, their glass reflecting the dim, moody light coming from unseen sources.
This place…keeps getting weirder…
*** thought as she slowly got to her feet. There was no point questioning it. She just had to
keep moving.
Find Sue. Find Agalika's soul.
As she passed the first mirror on her right tough, she couldn't help but peek at, half-expecting
to see it refuse to show her reflection at all.
But it did.
And it showed her exactly what a proper mirror would.
A gloomy-looking little girl in a torn black jacket and a once-prisitine dress, now smudged
with grime. Her long black hair had become a wild, tangled mess that would have made any
proper human mother despair—if she'd had a mother who cared about such things.
The bandages on her arms, applied with such care by Agalika, were dirty as well, spotted
with dust and older, dried blood. A fresh crust of it stained her chin.
"Why even bother?"
***'s heart skipped a beat.
Her mirrored self mouth had just moved on her own.
"Look at us.” The reflection sighed, slumping its shoulders. “Pathetic. Worthless. Couldn't do
anything good.”
Its grey eyes, dull with quiet resignation, met ***’s in the glass.
“Let’s lie down again. The floor's so inviting, isn't it? So much easier than walking..."*** backed away from the mirror, but in the process her heel caught on the edge of a worn
runner carpet.
She windmilled her arms, managing to turn the fall into a stumbling lurch toward the
opposite wall— and the mirror hanging there.
What she saw made her blood run cold.
A writhing, chaotic mass of glistening black tentacles filled the gilded frame, each appendage
a different size, their tips glowing with cold, dead starlight, all pressed urgently against the
other side of the glass as if trying to break through.
And in the center, where a body might have been, a single, great, unblinking eye stared out—
not at the hallway, but directly, at her.
"Фi`╤i╦KKK@dibvhf?" the tentacle-thing's voice rasped, broken, disjointed, like words
learned phonetically by a being with no concept of language.
"Фb╚KKK@d╢·q`w╢╚KKK@cfgw·fKKK@ФbvЁ`feKKK@svw╤KKK@╚Ёw·qf╚KKK"
***’s hands flew to her own face, her own torso, frantically patting, checking, confirming.
Two arms—only two.
A small, human nose.
Ribs that bent the right way.
One, two, three, four, five fingers on each hand.
No tentacles writhing from her back.
She was not that thing in the mirror that was her but couldn't be her because she'd changed,
she'd chosen, she'd transformed—
"Хw╢KKK@x·f`Ёfve…в·f`ЁfveKKK@i╢ubvKKK@c╢ЁKKK@╚ЁqttKKK@╢╚KKK"
With a choked cry, *** turned and fled, stumbling, skip-running down the long corridor,
desperate to escape them.
But they were everywhere now.
The hallway walls lined with more and more mirrors the more she run, and in every single
gilded frame, one of them waited for her.
"Give up—"
"Фi╦KKK@tfb╣fKKK@╢╚KKKo"
"So tired—"
"ФbvЁKKK@Ёw╢di…@г·quq…”╢
"Just rest—"
Down she went again, knees shrieking in protest at yet another unforgiving landing.
But it wasn’t wood nor polished marble what her palms made contact with—another mirror
—this one set into the floor like a decorative, ornate panel, a trap for the eyes.
What stared back up at her was neither the bedraggled human girl nor the monster of
tentacles and starlight.
Delicate.
That was the first word that came to ***'s mind.
A face softer than fresh rose petals, with a gentle oval jaw and a perfect button nose. Long,
shimmering hair cascaded down its back—not black, but the impossible color of new spring
leaves with bright sun shining through them.
Large, gentle eyes, the same soft, verdant green as the hair, blinked up at her. Dainty, pointed
ears, like a fairy's, peeked through the verdant strands.
And most striking of all were the wings—four of them, translucent and shimmering, catching
and refracting light that didn't even exist in the dim hallway. They looked as if they'd been
stolen from the largest, most beautiful dragonfly in all of creation.
This must have been how Thumbelina looked like after she'd gotten her wings. What the ugly
duckling must have seen when it finally recognized itself as a swan.
Beautiful. Perfect. Worth of love.
Everything *** wasn't.
“KYAHIHIHIHI!!!”
Suddenly, the fairy’s perfect, rose-petal lips curved into a smile that was all sharp edges and
did not reach those gentle, empty eyes.
“Look at you! Crawling on the floor like the pathetic little worm you are~!”
*** tried to push herself away, to scramble back, but her arms wouldn't obey, locked in place
by sheer horror.
“But Don't worry! Don't worry-worry!" The fairy-thing pressed both of its delicate hands flat
against the mirror's surface from below.  "Just accept what you really are—and you'll be
MAGNIFICENT!"
CRACK.
*** flinched, looking up. The first mirror – the one holding the despairing girl – had
fractured, its surface spiderwebbing.A small, pale hand pushed through the broken shards, followed by a head of tangled black
hair.
CRACK! CRACKLE!
From the other side, the tentacled monster pressed against its own mirror, the glass groaning,
bowing outwards before shattering, sending a shower of silver fragments across the floor as
the writhing mass of appendages began to slither out into the hallway.
CRACK!
A web of fine, iridescent cracks spread across the floor panel beneath *** as well, originating
from where the fairy’s hands pressed against the glass from the other side.
*** tried to scramble away again, but the fairy was faster.
Her willowy arms shot out, wrapping around *** from behind.
“Let’s be magnificent together~!”
The solid floor beneath became a cold, yielding surface, like plunging into freezing mercury.
The world of the dark, polished corridor stretched and distorted above her as she was
submerged.
Pulled inexorably down.
And down.
And down—
 
 
“NOOOOOO!!”
A scream tore itself from ***’s throat, raw and ragged with pure terror, as her back arched
violently off a hard, cool floor.
Her eyes flew open, wide and wild, darting frantically around, expecting to see the distorted,
receding image of the Victorian hallway.
Instead, her own terrified expression was mirrored back at her from the flawless, silver-lit
surface she lay upon.
Slowly, as realization dawned to her, the frantic, rabbit-fast hammering of her own borrowed
heart began to calm.
It was just a dream…Just… a bad dream…
Without really thinking about it, perhaps just to focus on something solid, something real that
didn’t make her tiny body shiver and tremble like a leaf, *** lifted the torn hem of her dress.
Her lower human limbs were a mess. Old bruises from her tour on the human world were still
visible—the most prominent the faded purple-yellow remnants on her shins from when that
orphan girl had tossed her to the ground to snatch the meat pie.
Alongside there were scrapes from cobblestones, a half-healed, puckered little cut from
broken glass during their frantic escape from Jimmy and that cursed wand.
And layered over those, like fresh paint on a decaying canvas, was new damage.
Fresh, angry bruises bloomed like dark, violent flowers where she'd crashed and fallen
repeatedly on the unyielding mirror floor. New scratches from her graceless landing after
escaping the ceiling trap.
Her knees were the worst—swollen, tender, painted in lurid shades of purple and red and raw
pink.
*** then lifted her small hands, studying her palms. The skin there was raw, scraped clean in
places, spotted with dried blood from too many falls.
And her fingernails… broken, chipped. Cracked like old, dry paint.
I could fix this.
Erase the pain that throbbed with every weary heartbeat.
Make myself whole and clean and presentable again.
Her hands began to tingle with that familiar, humming feeling of nascent power—
No.
She clenched her fists hard, extinguishing the feeling, letting the potential fade back into
nothingness. Pain flared in protest as her broken nails dug into her raw palms.
She welcomed it.
Not yet.
Not until I fixed what I broke.
Like the Little Mermaid, walking on knives to earn her place in the human world.
A soft, gentle glow interrupted her bleak resolution.
*** looked up.There, hovering just a few feet above her, a gentle white flame, no bigger than her fist,
bobbing patiently in the silent, sterile air.
Another soul.
It floated at the perfect height to look down at her, like the fairy godmother must have looked
when she first appeared before Cinderella, left to weep alone in the ashes after her stepsisters
had destroyed her dress.
A tiny, hesitant sound escaped ***'s lips.
"...Hi?"
The soul bobbed gently in response, saying nothing, simply waiting.
Hope, fragile and almost painful, pierced through her self-imposed misery.
“…Do you… can you bring me… to where your siblings are? Please? I need to find one.”
As if understanding completely, the white flame began to drift forward, away from her,
moving down the central corridor with a slow, deliberate, purposeful grace..
***’s heart leapt, a frantic, joyous flutter in her chest.
It was helping her! It understood!
With a pained grunt, she pushed herself up, using the mirrored wall for support.
Every step sent a fresh aching jolt shooting up her legs, but she ignored it, keeping her wide
grey eyes fixed on her gentle, silent guide, the small, bobbing flame of hope leading her
onward.
And the more they moved, the more the world began to change.
The infinite army of girls marching alongside *** started to waver. To fade, like ghosts
finally surrendering to the dawn, as the silver-slick surfaces grew cloudy, opaque. Dulling
until it resolved into solid, tangible texture of cool, grey marble, veined with darker lines like
frozen lightning.
Every now and then, new corridors, equally grand and silent, would appear on the left and
right.
From one, a pair of new souls would emerge.
From another, a single, brilliant reddish wisp would seep directly through the new marble
walls, joining their guide without a sound.
Soon the vast hallway was filled with them—dozens, perhaps hundreds, of gentle, silent
lights all flowing in the same inexorable direction. A silent, shimmering migration, a river of
lost stars flowing towards some unseen sea.One drifted close to ***, its placid white light bobbing just inches from her cheek. It seemed
to pulse with a faint, inviting curiosity.
And ***, unable to resist the overwhelming urge to know—
What are you?
How does it feel to the touch?
—reached out her small, bandaged hands, gently, carefully, cupping them around the floating
wisp of light.
It was neither warm nor cold—a strange, profound absence-cold, like touching the embers of
a bonfire long after the flames had died out.
The world shifted—
Wooden walls.
Thick, greasy smoke hanging in the air.
Sharp, sour-sweet reek of stale ale and unwashed bodies that made her—no, his—throat burn
in a way that was almost pleasant.
S—He was looking through someone else's eyes at a scarred, sticky bar counter.
An arm that wasn't hers—thick, corded with muscle, covered in coarse dark hair, the
knuckles swollen and scarred from old fights—lifted a heavy glass filled with amber poison.
"Been three whole days since Margaret left for her sister's…"
The glass touched lips that weren't hers, rough and chapped with cold. Tasted nothing as the
liquid vanished into a throat she didn't possess.
"Shoulda bet on the other fighter… bloody hell, that was… then I'd've had enough for more
than just one more dram—"
*** released the soul with a sharp gasp, stumbling backward and pressed her back against the
cool marble wall, blinking hard, ghost of cheap ale still stinging her throat.
What… what was that?
The soul, now freed, drifted placidly upward, rejoining its fellows in the silent, shimmering
river as if nothing had happened at all.
Still unsteady, *** pushed away from the wall, forcing herself to keep moving, to keep
following…
...Until another soul, perhaps drawn by the brief flicker of her own living energy, brushed
beneath her arm.Almost without thinking, a reflex born of sheer, disoriented curiosity, she caught it.
Her vision shifted again—
A lower perspective this time.
A warm room filled with bright, almost painfully vivid shapes that hurt to look at.
A tall green tree, covered in glowing glass ornaments and glittering tinsel.
Piles of brightly wrapped boxes sat in neat piles beneath it.
The vision changed—turning around—and a voice that wasn't hers, high and sweet and
bubbling with joy, spoke to two tall, smiling figures wreathed in warm light:
“Papa! Mama! Can I open one now? Please? Just one before breakfast?"
The tall humans made warm, laughing sounds of happy agreement.
Small hands that weren't hers, chubby and dimpled at the knuckles, reached eagerly for the
nearest box, the one wrapped in shiny red paper with a big green bow.
She felt a surge of anticipation building inside the small chest she now inhabited, mixed with
something else.
A certainty. The absolute, unshakeable knowledge that this was a special day, that this
mattered, that being here, between these two warm, smiling giants, was the safest, most
wonderful place in the entire universe.
The absolute certainty that being loved felt like—
"Iä-ghaaaa... tekeli-li-li-ghaaaa..."
A guttural, throaty voice, vast and cold as a glacier splitting, sliced through both the vision
and the fragile reality of the corridor like glass through silk.
*** let the soul slip away from her trembling hands, spinning with a frantic, animal instinct
toward the source of the noise.
Her mind tried to process what she saw, and came up with only disjointed fragments.
It was white. White as bone china, but flowing like silk in deep water. A perfect, featureless
sphere for a head, marked with a stark black cross that divided it into four precise sections,
like a compass rose or a target.
Where eyes should have been, two horizontal slashes gave it an expression of perpetual,
inhuman judgment.
It drifted toward her, trailing faint wisps of cyan light that made the air shimmer. It had no
visible legs, just a flowing, robed form that moved wrong, like smoke trying to be solid.And the hands—if they could be called hands—unnaturally long, with too many joints,
ending in fingers that looked carved from frozen breath, sharp as icicles.
"Tekeli-ghaaaa... iä-iä..."
The creature’s hands rose, gesturing—
Beckoning?
Pointing?
Inviting her judgment?
Two more identical creatures rounded a corner further ahead, gliding through the silent river
of souls without disturbing a single light.
Their arrival coincided with a sudden temperature drop. So drastic *** could see her own
breath clouding the air in thick, ragged plumes and frost formed on her eyelashes like tiny,
fragile crystals, stinging her eyes.
"Iä-ghaaaa... tekeli-li-li-ghaaaa..."
Terror, raw and primal, surged through her, a far more potent stimulant than any medicine.
The throbbing pain in her legs vanished beneath a flood of pure, blinding panic.
Run.
*** spun on her heel, stumble-bolting back the way she'd come. The grey marble corridor
ahead curved sharply, and gradually, the floor was mirror again, reflecting her terrified,
fleeing form into infinity.
The river of souls parted around her like a living curtain as she pushed through them with
both hands, each accidental touch bringing another fragment-flash of lives that weren't her—
—the sweet tang of birthday cake—
—the shy awkwardness of a first kiss—
—the crushing, hollow grief of a father's funeral—
—the overwhelming, miraculous joy of a baby's first cry—
"Tekeli-li-li... ghaaaa..."
*** risked a frantic glance back over her shoulder and immediately wished she hadn't.
The creatures moved faster than their serene drifting suggested possible, gliding through the
soul-river with a frightening, practiced ease.
The rightmost phantom's hands had opened fully.Reaching, reaching forward as if to—
Grab?
Touch?
Help?
—CRACK!
A mirror tile directly beneath her small Mary Janes splintered, and for a terrifying, infinite
moment, *** saw a thousand different girls, human and monster and monster and human and
nothing and everything.
All breaking apart together.
CRICK—CRACK!
The creatures behind her pulled up short, suddenly withdrawing.
"Kelite-GHAAA! Iä-iä-IÄ!"
Their guttural cries turned urgent, laced with something that might have been alarm, or
warning, or even a strange, alien anger.
*** will never know.
Because the ground wasn't there anymore.
Down through layers of glass and light and void she fell, the world blurring into streaks of
silver, white, and bottomless black.
Her torn dress billowed up around her, her long black hair streamed above her, reaching back
toward a sky that no longer existed.
It was dizzying. Disorienting. Absurd.
Maybe this is how Alice must have felt, tumbling down the rabbit hole.
Except here, there was no white rabbit to follow.
No tea party waiting below—
The chaotic, tumbling spiral downward ceased. Replaced instead by an odd sensation, a
feeling akin to being cradled gently like a baby bird fallen from its nest.
A feeling of being…
….held?
"Whatchya doin' there, little sparrow?"*** blinked, struggling to reorient, to make sense of up and down again. An old human face
peered down at her—the oldest, most ancient face she had ever seen.
His skin reminded her of pages in Yhounden's books after she’d crumpled them in her grief-
stricken rage a life ago. A magnificent white beard, fluffy-looking and soft as fresh-fallen
snow flowed down from his chin to his chest.
From within that web of wrinkles, his eyes peered out— sharp, clear grey things, crinkling at
the corners with what might have been genuine amusement.
He wore clothes like the fancy gentlemen she'd glimpsed in London's Hyde Park, a suit of
dark adorned with golden buttons that seemed to pulse with a soft, internal warmth, as if they
were breathing.
But he was so… small.
Even lying cradled in his arms, *** could tell he was barely any taller than her own child-
form.
"Wh... who... huh... what...?"
How could a old ordinary human be here at all?
The old man let out a sound like a handful of smooth, dry pebbles being danced in a tin cup
—a series of rough, rattling cackles that echoed strangely in the vast, quiet space around
them.
“Saved you from quite the nasty landing, missy,” He chuckled, and with movements as gentle
and precise as someone placing a priceless piece of porcelain on a shelf, he set her carefully
on her feet. “You're welcome."
*** swayed for a moment, still dizzy, her small hands coming up to steady herself. Only then
did she truly take in her new surroundings.
They were…In a library?
If it was really a library, it was unlike anything from her stories.
Shelves, carved from some azure, unidentifiable stone, stretched up into a darkness so
complete, so absolute, it might have been solid matter, disappearing into an unseen ceiling
that could have been miles away.
And the books… they were filled not with paper and ink, but with what looked like captured
starlight, each volume glowing softly from within, casting a gentle, multi-hued light on its
neighbors.
"Was just doing a little constitutional, a little walk around these parts myself, you see…” The
old man spoke again, stroking his magnificent white beard between his thumb and forefinger
"...And I seem to have gotten myself a touch lost."He looked back at her, and despite the fact he barely reached her shoulder, something about
his eyes made *** want to take a reflexive step backward.
"You wouldn't mind walking a tired old man back to his little corner of this place, would you,
my dear?”
*** hesitated, glancing between the strange petite old human before he and the infinite
darkness above—where she'd fallen from, where the river of souls flowed.
Sue and Agalika are still waiting…
Every second wasted here was a second they remained lost.
A second Agalika's soul might fade forever.
“Uhm... I’m…I’m already busy... I have to find someone..."
”Ah! I see, I see! The old man chuckled again. “A fine and good reason, that one.”
No disappointment in his voice, only a kind of cheerful acceptance that felt strangely…
rehearsed?
“Then by all means, go ahead, young lady. Waste no more time on my account.” He gestured
with one of his blood-red gloves towards a path that wound away behind him, between two
towering shelves.
“That path there should get you back upstairs, closer to where you fell from. As for me…
well, I'll just handle myself on my own."
A wave of relief washing over ***.
She was excused.
She could continue her mission.
Already turning toward the mentioned path, she took a step. Her Mary Janes clicked on the
floor of deep azure marble that felt more real, more solid, than anything she’d stood on since
entering this dream-like place.
She took another three steps.
Another.
Then stopped.
A image rose, unbidden and horribly vivid: this frail old human, shuffling alone through the
silent corridors, suddenly meeting one of those flowing white phantoms. Their long, icy
fingers reaching for his papery, wrinkled skin.He'd shatter like the mirrors had, wouldn't he? Sliced into a thousand pathetic pieces of fancy
cloth and golden buttons and brittle old bones.
I already failed to save one person who'd shown her kindness…
Am I really going to let another one walk to their doom…?
Slowly, reluctantly, *** turned back.
The old man hadn't moved, still standing in the same spot she left him. His gaze was set
downward, those gloved hands of his busy adjusting something golden that hung on a chain
around his neck.
A medallion? A pocket watch?
She couldn't tell from this distance, but it gleamed with its own soft, internal light.
He must have heard her steps approaching, for he looked up just as she got right in front of
him.
“Mm? Changed your mind?" His eyes crinkled deeper at the corners, and for a second, ***
thought she saw a flicker of sly delight briefly dancing in those too-sharp grey grids. “If so,
then you have this old man's eternal gratitude, young maiden."
*** shifted uncomfortably from foot to foot. She didn't want his gratitude. Gratitude felt like
just another kind of debt, and she already owed far too much to far too many.
But... 'In fairy tales...' a sweet voice that sounded suspiciously like the kind elk, when you
stop to help lost old people on your journey, sometimes they're secretly fairy godmothers. Or
powerful wizards. Or even gods in disguise, testing the hearts of mortals.
Maybe… maybe this wasn’t a detour?
Maybe this was the path?
Maybe he knew where souls were kept in this strange place?
Maybe he’d seen where Sue fell?
Maybe, maybe, maybe...
"Where..." *** finally whispered, her voice barely disturbing the azure-tinted shadows of the
library. "where is your place?"
"Oh, just somewhere around here, I'm quite certain of it! Don't you worry your pretty head.
These old bones of mine remember the way, they just… need a bit of a walk to jog the
memory, you see."
He began shuffling forward with movements that seemed far too smooth, too fluid, for
someone who looked ready to crumble into dust."Come along! Come Along!” he called cheerfully over his shoulder. "Two sets of eyes are
always better than one, especially when one set has seen as many winters as mine have!"
With a final, hesitant glance back towards the path that supposedly led to her own quest, ***
sighed and fell into step beside him.
*** hoped Sue would understand.
*** hoped Agalika's soul would wait.
*** hoped helping one lost, strange old man wouldn't end up costing her everything else.
…But then again, when had hope ever really helped ***?

With that said...see ya next time! And stay safe!Chapter 11: Between the Lines
The silence felt different.
Not the silence of absence—more like sound itself was being swallowed by the towering
shelves on either side, rising into a darkness so total it might have been carved from the end
of time.
And there, In the space amidst them, ***’s small, aching feet clicked softly on the deep azure
marble, crisp and clean—a sharp relief after the nauseating silence of the mirror maze, where
footsteps had arrived too early or too late.
Here, it seemed at least, the world at last yielded under the laws of gravity. Sound. Time.
She trailed just a step behind the strange old human male, the latter shuffling forward with
unsettlingly fluid gait, coat tails whispering behind him, bandaged hands clasped tight behind
her back, unconsciously mimicking his posture, while her wide grey eyes drank in the world..
At first, the shelves held a facade of order.
Right angles….
Straight lines….
Books in tidy, logical rows.
But the farther they walked, the more the architecture began to unravel, bending inward at
nonsensical curves. Perspectives twisted like pulled taffy.
One aisle seemed built entirely from rhombuses, stacked in paradox—corners kissing itself at
angles a human could never create. The scrolls tucked into those angular gaps pulsed brighter
than the rest: deep violet, urgent crimson, angry blue. Light spilling from them pooled on the
floor like liquified starfire.
“Quite the collection, isn’t it?”
The Old Man’s voice—dry as autumn leaves—broke ***'s trance.
"Mhm..."
Unlike ***, He didn’t seem bothered in the slightest by the alien architecture, merely
gesturing lazily with one red-gloved hand toward a shelf that appeared…
...Inside-out…?
The books there clung to surfaces that should have been the shelf’s hidden back.“Been here longer than I can remember…” He murmured, stroking his glorious beard. “…Or
maybe I’m just so old I forgot, Gyahaha!!”
*** nodded absently, her gaze still devoured by the sight unfolding left and right.
Forget the strange angles. the physics-defying shelves.
Story-holders.
Books.
Millions.
More.
More than she had ever imagined.
In comparison, ***’s little library, born from the Kind Elk’s generous gifts, once the very
heart of ***’s world, felt like a pebble compared to this…this infinite beach.
Slowly, without even realising it, her path began to drift, her steps pulling her left, away from
the Old Man, carrying her closer, and closer still, to the glowing azure shelves on her left.
There, a tome pulsed with a soft, inviting emerald light, like the wing of green fairy.
What stories did this one hold?
Knights from dying stars?
Her fingers rose, trembling.
To feel it.
To know.
Princesses who could command thunders?
To guess the story from its pulse—
Warmth intensified against her skin.
Maybe… maybe things like me?
Her fingertips grazed the air just an inch from its glowing cover.
Things who wanted—
“'And yours Malishka?'”
*** yanked her hand back as if burned.Stumbled.
One step.
Two.
Then slammed both palms to her chest, clutching hard at the frantic drumbeat behind her ribs.
Stupid.
Stupid….STUPID…selfish thing….!
Eyes squeezed shut. *** shook her head once, hard. Hands clenching into a fist so tight her
broken nails bit into her raw palms.
They’re waiting.
Sue—lost, maybe hurt.
Agalika—fading.
And you…
You dare…
DARE…
“Found something interesting?”
*** jolted, spinning around so fast her bad knees nearly buckled. The Old Man stood right
there, closer than her own shadow, those sharp grey eyes  of crinkling at the corners.
Hadn’t he been further down the aisle?
How did He…?
His gloved hand darted past her face. Fast. Precise. He plucked the emerald book from the
shelf with a magician’s ease, holding it up like a fish caught by the gills.
“My, my… went straight for a thick one...”
He brought it close to his face, turning it left, right, up to the half-light.
“Heh, You’ve got a strong arm for such a little reader.”
"H-how—?"
He shoved the book into her chest.
*** gasped, clutching it reflexively."Take it."
He turned back to the path and resumed that shuffling onward walk while *** stood frozen.
The glowing volume hummed against her sternum.
Take it?
Now?
"T-this..." She glanced down at the glowing book, then up at the old human’s retreating back.
"This is not... the time to read..."
"Maybe not, maybe not! But nothing says you can’t do it later on!" He glanced over his
shoulder. One eyebrow arched up. "Besides..."
His grey, grid-like eyes narrowed.
"I saw the way you were looking at it.... You're a bookworm, aren't ya?"
Book...'worm'?
***’s gaze dropped down.
Me?
She checked her skin. No segments. No slime. Just ribs and the rough fabric of the dress
covering ***'s modest curves.

He wiggled the offended finger. A barely visible shard caught the light, surrounded by a
perfect bead of blood that gleamed like a tiny ruby.
"If you are not a coward, give me your best shot and pull it out."
*** stared at the offered hand.
Such a small thing.
"F-Fine..."
She reached for his hand.
Hesitated.
Her own fingers trembled slightly.
"Go on, go on~"
The Old Man wiggled his finger again, the glass catching light with each movement.
"...Unless you're scared of a little glass too?"
That did it.
*** positioned herself carefully, hovering her palms near his hand without touching, and
closed her eyes, reaching for the pressure.
In her mind, she built the image of the tiny shard. A sliver of sharp light, barely there but
definitely present.
She imagined gripping it between invisible fingers.
Delicate. Careful.
Like picking up a butterfly's wing.
"Oh, blast it... butterfingers striking again..."
One smooth motion—
She raised her hand in a gripping gesture, pulling upward with careful force.
The splinter resisted more than she expected.
Much more.Why is glass so heavy...?
*** frowned, applying more power.
Yet resistance grew proportionally, as if the tiny shard was anchored to something massive.
GRRRRRRIND—
Stone scraping against stone rumbled somewhere a few meters forward.
But that couldn't be right.
Glass didn't sound like—
GRRRRRRRIND—
Louder now. Vibrating through the floor, up into her knees.
Chirrup-chirrup!
At her feet, the lampies began chirping.
Excited, encouraging sounds.
Beep-beep!
***'s brow furrowed deeper, and for a moment, she was tempted to open her eyes. To see
what was happening.
Why the lampies sounded so pleased.
Why the floor was shaking—
No.
Don't.
If I lose focus again, I'll ruin everything. Again.
She kept her eyes squeezed shut. Kept pulling.
...Maybe it went all the way through his finger?
The grinding became a roar.
This glass is special or what?
How does it make that—
THUMP.Resistance vanished all at once.
A gust of air hit her face, carrying dust and the scent of old paper and candlewax, tickled her
nose, building, building—
"Ah... AH-CHOO!"
***'s eyes flew open.
She found herself standing not in front of the Old Man and his injured finger, but facing a
wide-open passage where the marble wall had been.
Beyond it, a continuation of the hallway beckoned with promise. Golden light and the
suggestion of something waiting at its end.
"Well, would you look at that!"
*** flinched. Again. Spinning toward the voice.
The Old Man stood right beside her. A triumphant smile crinkling every wrinkle on his worn
out skin.
And most telling of all, his glove was back on his hand, fitting perfectly, as if it had never
been removed.
"Excellent work, young missy!" He clapped his hands together. "That body of yours may be
tiny, but you're sure stronger in many other places!"
"I... but... the glass..."
*** stumbled toward him, reaching for his hand with shaking fingers.
"Your finger... the splinter..."
She grabbed his gloved hand, turning it over desperately.
Through the fabric, she could feel his index finger beneath.
Whole.
Uninjured.
No bump where glass should be embedded.
"Oh, that?" The Old Man's eyes crinkled deeper.
"Managed to get it out myself while you were concentrating. Popped right out! These old
bones of mine still manage to give me surprises now and then."
*** stared at him.Then at the open wall.
Then back at him.
Tap-tap-tap-tap—
Two of the lampies were already climbing her dress, hooked claws finding familiar purchase
on their way back to her shoulders.
The third launched itself upward and landed with a soft thud atop her head, settling into her
tangled hair with a satisfied chirp.
"You..."
She let go of his hand and stepped back.
"You tricked me!"
"'Tricked you'?"
The Old Man placed a gloved hand over his heart.
"Such a harsh, crude word!" "I'd rather call it... 'redirecting your expectations.'"
He took a step closer then, and before *** could retreat further, his gloved hand came down
gently on her shoulder.
Pat. Pat.
"Look—you said you couldn't open it because it was too heavy... yet, here we are."
He gestured grandly at the open passage with his walking stick.
"All it took was untangling that young mind of yours from hesitation and doubt."
*** opened her mouth to argue.
Closed it.
Opened it again, like a fish trying to breathe air.
"That's... That's mean."
"Gyagya! Perhaps! But as some other old bones once said—the means justify the end..."
His gaze darted past ***'s shoulder, fixing on something behind her.
For just a flicker—barely a heartbeat—genuine surprise crossed those weathered features,
only to melt just as quickly into a grin."And you got it open just in time, too. That nice young fellow behind you is just raring to go
through."
"Nice... young..."
*** frowned in confusion.
What is he suddenly talking about? What fellow?
We're alone in this strange place—
Chirrrrrr...
The three lampies on her shoulders and head went rigid, emanating a low, wary hum, their
gaze aimed at something behind her.
*** turned around.
White flooded her sight.
White like bone china, but flowing like silk underwater.
A perfect, featureless sphere for a head, marked with a stark black cross that divided it into
four precise sections.
Where eyes should have been, two horizontal slashes gave it an expression of perpetual,
inhuman judgment.
No.
Not this again...!
She took an instinctive step back, nearly stumbling over her own feet, while the creature's
head tilted—A motion too smooth, too mechanical—And those line-eyes seemed to focus
directly on her.
Its hands rose then. Claw-fingers, unnaturally long, with too many joints, spread wider, blue
flames bloomed between.
"Oh dear!"
*** risked a glance sideways towards the old man, finding that the latter had moved
backward until his back bumped lightly against the nearby wall, both hands folded atop his
walking stick.
"Best dodge that, little miss."
As on cue, the creature flung its arms forward. Blue flames screamed through the air.
Not fire, ***'s mind registered in the fraction of a second before instinct took over, because
fire was warm and these were cold.So cold the air crystallized in their wake—
*** threw herself sideways, slamming her shoulders against the wall hard enough to rattle
her teeth.
The flames passed where she'd been standing, splashing against the opposite wall in a
cascade of frost, turning the bricks into ice that slipped and fell on the floor.
Chirp-CHIRP-CHIRRRP!
All three lampies hurled themselves into the air from her shoulders, from her head, with
furious battle cries.
Mid-flight, their limbs retracted, snapping into perfect spheres, and threw themselves at the
ghost like angry glowing cannonballs.
"No, wait—!"
Too late.
The creature's arm swept sideways in a lazy, almost dismissive arc.
THWACK!
One lampy, the one that had been on ***'s head, flew away, bouncing twice before rolling
into a shadowed corner near the far wall.
The other two went spinning through the air, tumbling end over end, their startled chirps
dopplering away—
The Old Man caught them.
One in each gloved hand, plucked from the air with an ease that had no business belonging to
someone whose appearances screamed "I'm old!"
"Well, well~ Finally got to hold you, my little fellows!"
GRRRRR-
Beep-beep-
GRRRR!
Both lampies squirmed furiously in his grip, legs flailing in furious but useless kicks, lights
flickering with indignation.
"Now, now~ Best let the young miss handle the big guy, yes?"
More blue flames gathered between those too-long fingers, causing the air to shimmer with a
glacial cold that shouldn't exist in a library, making ***'s borrowed lungs burn with every
breath."P-please... stop!"
"I don't want to fight!"
The creature didn't react in the slightest. Not even an acknowledgment that she'd spoken at
all.
Its arms swept down, releasing another volley of frozen projectiles.
*** threw herself into a roll, the ice-blades shattering against the wall behind her in starbursts
of pale light.
Frost crunched beneath her palms as she scrambled upright, backing toward the open
passage.
Think.
Think.
In fairy tales, how do you stop a monster without fighting?
Kindness. Compliments.
Finding the good in them—
"Your flames!"
The white ghost's arm paused mid-sweep.
"T-they're—they're very..."
***'s mind scrambled for the right word.
"P-pretty...?"
The ghost went still.
Its sphere-head tilted, its crisscross line-eyes somehow conveying pure confusion despite
having no features to express it, and for one breathless moment, the blue flames between its
fingers dimmed.
It... it really worked?
Maybe if I keep—
The ghost's posture snapped back into aggression.
More projectiles. Faster this time.
*** barely ducked the first wave, felt the second graze her hair, heard the third shatter against
the wall inches from her ear.No, no, it ALMOST worked!
She dodged another barrage while her mind raced.
The creature moved in specific patterns
Sweep of arms.
Gathering power.
Release.
It resembled a little dance.
So when the ghost swept its right arm, *** swept left.
When it glided backward, *** glided forward.
When it raised both hands, *** raised both hands.
After the umpteenth mimicry, the creature slowed its movements. Until it stopped still, and its
sphere-head turned.
Just for a heartbeat.
A flicker of motion that might have been nothing. Past her, where the Old Man stood holding
the two squirming lampies.
Then its sphere-head swiveled away, locking its line-eyed gaze not on *** entirely, but
something else.
Something in the far corner of the room.
The third lampy.
It lay on its side, lights flickering weakly, one limb stuck beneath a brick, twitching in a futile
attempt to right itself.
The ghostly monster raised both arms, blue flames and sharp projectiles manifesting
simultaneously, aiming all that frozen arsenal at one tiny, defenseless thing.
Like its mere existence offended the ghost somehow.
This is going to hurt.
*** ran toward the defenceless lampy and dove, sliding across the frozen floor, scooping the
tiny creature against her chest.
This is going to hurt so much...!
She curled around it, making herself as small as possible,Bracing for impact—
Nothing came.
Silence.
*** waited, trembling, clutching the little Lampy tighter against her hammering chest.
Still nothing.
Slowly, hesitantly, she uncurled enough to peek over her shoulder.
The ghost hovered exactly where it had been. But the blue flames were gone. Its claw-fingers
relaxed.
Even the harsh cyan-white of its form dimmed.
Suddenly It drifted forward, passing right by ***, close enough she could have touched that
flowing form, and it was gone. Vanishing down the revealed hallway like it had never been
there at all.
Frost slowly began melted into puddles. Brick fragments finished their clattering descent.
The lampy in ***'s arms let out a tiny, grateful chirp.
Beep!
"Whew! That sure was a trip!"
The Old Man released the two lampies he'd been holding. They immediately scrambled
toward ***, and together with their sibling they climbed up her dress, settling back above her.
"After you, my brave assistant!"
He positioned himself beside the newly opened entrance.
"My study is closer than ever, and you've proven yourself quite the lady, my dear."
Assistant?
Lady?
She almost died.
Or at least, her human body almost got very cold and full of holes.
And he's acting like they just finished a pleasant walk.
Still, she approached on unsteady legs, the three lampies pressed tight against her shoulders
and head, their lights slowly brightening back to normal.The one she'd saved, the one that had been knocked into the corner, kept nuzzling against her
neck, making soft grateful sounds.
Beep. Chirrup. Beep-beep.
"Shall we?"  The Old Man gestured toward the golden-lit hallway with his walking stick. "I
do believe you mentioned looking for someone, and time has a way of slipping by when
you're dodging spectral projectiles."
*** hurried to keep up. The clicking of the Old Man's cane set a steady rhythm as they
walked, her Mary Janes splashing through rapidly forming puddles where frost had melted.
After the chaos of before, dodging, diving, frost and shattered brick, the peaceful corridor felt
almost wrong.
Like the calm in fairy tales right before the witch reveals her true form.
But minutes passed and nothing leapt out.
Just their footsteps echoing off walls that shifted from damaged brick to smooth stone to
something that might be polished wood. 
The lampies gradually relaxed, their tense grips loosening, their worried chirps fading into
contented silence.
"...Tell me something, little miss."
The Old Man's voice suddenly broke the quiet.
"Why did you try to save that little fellow?"
*** blinked, confused by the question.
Her hand rose unconsciously to touch the lampy at her neck. It pressed back against her palm,
warm despite its cold shell.
"Because... it was going to get hurt? That creature was about to—" She gestured vaguely. "It
would have... been destroyed."
"Hmm..."
The Old Man studied her face like he was reading a book written in a language he almost
understood.
"...I mean, that spectral fellow sure had quite the grudge against your little friend... Forgot all
about you...."
He nodded toward the Lampy.
"You could have used that to your advantage."He made a vague gesture with his free hand.
"Done the same thing you did with the wall, perhaps.... A little push here, a little pull there..."
Memories flashed behind ***'s eyes before she could stop it.
The carriage flying through the air. Agalika's body, wrong wrong wrong.
The sound of breaking.
The crowd screaming.
Blood on cobblestones.
Empty eyes staring at nothing—
"...I didn't want to."
Please don't ask more.
Please just let it go.
The Old Man watched her for another heartbeat—Something flickered in those sharp grey
eyes—before he nodded solemnly.
"Heh. Suppose that's right. Suppose that's right..."
They walked in silence for a few more steps.
*** counted the clicks of his cane.
One, two, three, four.
"By the way—thank you again for your assistance, little miss. I wouldn't be here without
you."
He glanced down at her.
"May you grace me with your name at last? It's only fair I get to know how my benefactor is
called, after all."
A theatrical sigh.
"And honestly, 'little miss' is growing rather tedious."
*** shifted uncomfortably.
The last time she'd tried to say her name—
"I..."She fidgeted with a torn edge of her dress, then whispered so quietly even she barely heard it.
"...My name is... ***."
"Mmm...?"
The Old Man leaned closer, cupping his free hand around his ear.
"Could you please repeat?"
*** sighed, raising her voice just a little.
"My name is ***."
"I really am sorry, but could you repeat again?"
He tapped his ear apologetically, face arranged into an expression of genuine distress.
"You see, these ears of mine are feeling quite mischievous right now."
Frustration bubbled up in ***'s chest. Hot and tight and impossible to swallow.
Before she could stop herself—
"MY NAME IS ***!"
The lampies startled on her shoulders, letting out alarmed chirps—
chirp-chirp-CHIRP!
—flickering their lights in surprise.
Too loud...!
Horror crashed through *** like ice water.
I said it too loud!
Her eyes squeezed shut.
Any second now he'll fall.
His knees will buckle.
And it will be my fault again.
Always my fault—
"***?"
***'s eyes flew open.He stood exactly where he'd been, one eyebrow raised, an amused grin slowly curving his sry
lips.
No signs of distress, no blood either.
"My, a peculiar name, to be sure!"
He threw his head back and let out his signature rough, rattling laugh.
"Gyahaha! What am I saying? All names are interesting when you ponder them long
enough!"
He resumed walking, cane clicking cheerfully against the floor.
He's... fine?
The lampies had settled again, their startled chirps fading back into quiet contentment. They
didn't seem hurt either. No flickering. No distress.
Her feet moved on their own, hurrying to catch up.
Why?
Is there something different about this human?
She trailed behind, trying to puzzle it out.
Maybe... maybe it's because we're not in the human world?
But no—that doesn't make sense. Sound is sound, no?
Unless... unless there's something about this place that changes how voices work?
Or maybe—
Her thoughts scattered as they stopped before a door.
Carvings curled and twisted across its surface in patterns that almost made sense before
slipping away into meaninglessness. Brass hinges gleamed despite the age that seemed to
seep from every grain.
The Old Man produced a key.
*** didn't see where exactly—one moment his hand was empty, the next it held an ornate
brass key, its bow shaped like something between a snowflake and a spider's web.
He fit it into a lock hidden among the carvings.
Click.
The door swung open silently, and from within, golden light spilled, warm and inviting."Ladies first."
He stepped aside, gesturing grandly with his walking stick.
Is there any difference if a female or a male goes inside somewhere first?*
** hesitated at the threshold.
Maybe... maybe he's trying to use me as bait?
Like how I threw pebbles to find the hungry black mouths?
The thought should have bothered her more than it did.
But right now, it didn't matter.
None of this mattered.
"I... I did my part. I helped you find your way back."
*** took a step back from the doorway, already glancing down the corridor they'd come
from.
Searching for any other path.
Anything that could lead her back up—back to the place where she'd fallen.
"...I need to go now, do... the things I came here for."
Find Agalika's soul.
Fix everything.
Make the pain stop.
"Ah, of course, of course! Silly me, how could I forget!"
The Old Man straightened from his bow, nodding sagely. Not a trace of disappointment on
that weathered face. Not even surprise.
"Well then, I wish you the very best of luck my dear brave little ***."
With that said he turned around, passing through the other side of the doorway, coat tails
whispering behind him, that familiar tap-tap-tap of his walking stick fading into the golden
glow beyond.
*** watched him go for a few seconds before turning her back on the study, approaching the
nearby right wall.
Her palms pressed against cold stone, searching for seams, for cracks, for any hint of a secret
passage—"HEY! Already back, huh?!"
***'s hands froze against the wall.
"Pfffft! What? What?—you really thought locking me up for a few hours would break my
spirit?! HA! Think again, dusty oldy bonebag!"
The voice cracked with defiance. With the bratty, unbreakable energy of someone who would
rather chew off their own arm than admit defeat.
...Sue?
She spun around.
